Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in. Um, well, I hope I'm in the right place, and I really don't want to sound like I'm just complaining or being lazy... but I came in because I’ve just been so tired. Well, all the time, really. 

I know everyone gets tired, and I always try my best to just push through it, but... by midmorning at work—I'm a claims adjuster—after just reviewing a few files or, you know, walking a junior adjuster through a claim, it just feels like my body and my brain are already completely running on empty.

Oh, um... well, it's been quite a while, actually. I feel a little embarrassed admitting it because I probably should have come in sooner, but... I'd say it's been going on for about three years. Maybe three years and three months? 

It started right around the time they moved me into a higher-responsibility role at work. They asked me to start mentoring the other adjusters and overseeing the new workflow systems. Which was a wonderful opportunity, really, and I'm so grateful they trusted me with it! I don't want it to sound like I'm complaining about the promotion or that I can't handle it. I just... I guess that's when I really started noticing how drained I was feeling. 

I kept thinking I would eventually just get used to the new pace and bounce back, you know? I hope it's okay that I waited this long to bring it up... is it normal to take that long to adjust to a change like that?

Oh, um... well, I really hate to say yes, because I don't want to sound ungrateful. I have a wonderful life, I really do. But... I suppose I have been feeling a bit down. 

It's just... most days, I feel like there's a sort of gray film over everything? I mean, I still do everything I need to do! I go through the motions and make sure everyone is taken care of, and I always try to keep a smile on my face. But even when my husband is being so sweet and kind, or we're all cooking together as a family—which are things I know I should be so happy about—I just... I don't really feel the enjoyment I used to. 

I feel awful even saying that out loud. I know I should just be happy with what I have, and I really try my best not to let them see it so I don't bring anyone else down. Does that make me sound like a terrible person? I just want to be sure I'm not making too big a deal out of nothing...

I haven't been doing those things as much. \n\nI just feel so bad because I'm barely getting through the week, you know? I'm not just being lazy, am I?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling down or sad?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I suppose it's really been about the same amount of time as the tiredness? I'm so sorry, I wish I had a more exact date for you. I really don't want to be unhelpful. \n\nIt's just... I think it all kind of started together, right around that three-year mark when work changed. I kept trying so hard to push through it and put on a brave face for everyone, so I didn't really let myself keep track of when exactly the sad feelings started. I just kept telling myself I was fine, so it all sort of blends together. It just kind of... became my normal, if that makes sense? \n\nI hope that's an okay answer... I know you probably need specifics for your notes. Am I giving you the right information? I can try to think harder about it if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh... um. Well, I feel a little embarrassed to admit this, because I really do try to stay active and involved. But... yes, I suppose so. I used to really love painting, and hiking, and I used to volunteer a lot, too. But lately, I've sort of pulled back from all of that. I'm really just trying to get through the week, to be honest. 

And, um... I hate to even mention this part, because I pride myself on my work, but... I've noticed I'm taking a bit longer to sign off on my files lately. And I might have missed a couple of tiny details in my recent audits. It's nothing huge, I promise! I'm still doing my absolute best to keep everything running smoothly, and nobody has complained. I just... I don't want you to think I'm slacking off or anything. I just don't seem to have the energy for my hobbies right now because I'm trying so hard to keep my head above water at the office. 

Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding lazy... I really am trying to push through it.

Oh, gosh, yes... definitely more than two months. I feel like I'm making it sound so dramatic, but... it's actually been a lot longer than that. 

I'd say it's been about, um, three years and three months? Give or take. It started right around the time I got promoted at work. I just kept thinking I should be able to push through it on my own, you know? Is that a normal timeline for people to feel this way?

Like, even at work—and I hate admitting this because I really pride myself on doing a good job—but I've started missing some small details during my audits, and it's taking me a little longer to sign off on files. I'm trying so hard to fix it, I really am. I just... I don't want to let anyone down. Is it okay that I'm struggling with this? I'm not just being lazy, am I?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling down or sad?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I suppose it's really been about the same amount of time as the tiredness? I'm so sorry, I wish I had a more exact date for you. I really don't want to be unhelpful. \n\nIt's just... I think it all kind of started together, right around that three-year mark when work changed. I kept trying so hard to push through it and put on a brave face for everyone, so I didn't really let myself keep track of when exactly the sad feelings started. I just kept telling myself I was fine, so it all sort of blends together. It just kind of... became my normal, if that makes sense? \n\nI hope that's an okay answer... I know you probably need specifics for your notes. Am I giving you the right information? I can try to think harder about it if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really try to find the good in things, I do. I don't want to sound like I'm just complaining about everything. \n\nBut, um... to be completely honest, I can't really think of anything specific right now that I genuinely look forward to. I mean, I still do things! I still cook dinner and I still go to work, and I try to make sure everything runs smoothly for my family. But that feeling of excitement, or... or really enjoying something? I just haven't felt that in a while. \n\nI'm so sorry, that sounds so depressing, doesn't it? I know I should be more positive. I really am trying. Is it bad that I don't have a better answer for you? I hope I'm not making your job too difficult...\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, I haven't. Um... I'm so sorry if I made it sound like things were *that* bad. I know I've been complaining a lot today, and I really don't want you to worry about me in that way. \n\nI promise I would never do anything to hurt myself. I'm still trying my best for my husband and my family, and I just want to figure out how to feel like myself again. I hope I didn't alarm you... I'm just having a hard time, but I'm definitely not at that point. \n\nI hope that's a relief to hear? I just really want to get back on track, I'm not giving up.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, not really. I mean, I think my eating and sleeping have been pretty normal? \n\nI know some people get really off track with those things when they're stressed, but I try really hard to keep a good routine. I still make sure to eat three meals a day, and I'm in bed at a reasonable hour. I don't want to let things fall apart, you know? \n\nSometimes I might wake up a little early, or maybe I don't finish everything on my plate, but nothing drastic. Nothing that would make you worry, I promise. I hope that's helpful? I'm trying to be as accurate as I can for you.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the tiredness and the gray film over everything, without any significant improvement?\n\nPatient: Oh, wow... um, let me think. I don't want to exaggerate it, because I know people go through much worse. But, um... I guess it's been a while now. \n\nMaybe... about three years? Oh, goodness, maybe three years and three months, if I'm being exact. It started right around the time they promoted me at work. They gave me this higher-responsibility role, mentoring the other adjusters and overseeing all the new workflow systems. \n\nIt's a great opportunity, it really is! I should be so grateful for it, and I am. But I think I just... I haven't quite bounced back since then. I've just been pushing through. I hope I don't sound ungrateful for the promotion... I really do try to do a good job for them. Does that make sense, that it would start around a big change like that?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now feel neutral or unenjoyable to you?\n\nPatient: Oh... um. Well, I feel a little embarrassed to admit this, because I really do try to stay active and involved. But... yes, I suppose so. I used to really love painting, and hiking, and I used to volunteer a lot, too. But lately, I've sort of pulled back from all of that. I'm still doing my absolute best to keep everything running smoothly, and nobody has complained. I just... I don't want you to think I'm slacking off or anything. I just don't seem to have the energy for my hobbies right now because I'm trying so hard to keep my head above water at the office. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding lazy... Oh, um... I'm so sorry, I feel like I'm making you ask the same question twice! I really don't mean to be frustrating. But... yes, it's been the whole time.
